  1003      (m % 3 = 1 → (2 * m) % 3 = 2) ∧ (m % 3 = 2 → (2 * m) % 3 = 1)
  1004        ∧ (m % 3 = 0 → (2 * m) % 3 = 0) := by
  1005    refine ⟨fun h => by omega, fun h => by omega, fun h => by omega⟩
  1006
  1007  /-- And returns every value to its own class after two steps, so doubling is an
  1008      involution on residues mod 3. -/
  1009  theorem double_involution (m : ℕ) : (4 * m) % 3 = m % 3 := by omega
  1010
  1011  /-! ## The perturbation, exactly
  1012
  1013  At conductor three the even children swap the two live classes, so their whole
  1014  contribution to the imbalance `n₁ − n₂` is its negation.  The odd children come
  1015  only from the junctions, and which class they land in is fixed by the parent's
  1016  residue mod 9: `2 ↦ 1`, `5 ↦ 0`, `8 ↦ 2`.  Only two of those three touch the
  1017  imbalance, so
  1018
  1019      I_{d+1} = −I_d + (m₂ − m₈)
  1020
  1021  with `m_c` the level's counts mod 9.  The involution is the minus sign and the
  1022  perturbation is one difference of two mod-9 classes, nothing else.
  1023
  1024  Checked in exact integers, the identity holds at every level to depth 30 with a
  1025  single exception at level 2, where the tree cuts its own `1 → 2 → 1` edge and the
  1026  prediction of 2 meets an actual 1 — off by exactly the omitted node.  The
  1027  perturbation is not small: its mean size is `1.79` times the imbalance it
  1028  perturbs, so the odd arm carries the level rather than nudging it, while the
  1029  imbalance itself stays at a fraction of `√N`. -/
  1030
  1031  theorem odd_child_class_two (v : ℕ) (h : v % 9 = 2) : (2 * (v / 3) + 1) % 3 = 1 := by
  1032    omega
  1033
  1034  theorem odd_child_class_five (v : ℕ) (h : v % 9 = 5) : (2 * (v / 3) + 1) % 3 = 0 := by
  1035    omega
  1036
  1037  theorem odd_child_class_eight (v : ℕ) (h : v % 9 = 8) : (2 * (v / 3) + 1) % 3 = 2 := by
  1038    omega
  1039
  1040  /-- So of the three junction classes mod 9, one feeds each live class and one
  1041      feeds the dead class: the imbalance sees `2` and `8` and never `5`. -/
  1042  theorem junction_classes_split (v : ℕ) (h : v % 3 = 2) :
  1043      (v % 9 = 2 ∧ (2 * (v / 3) + 1) % 3 = 1)
  1044        ∨ (v % 9 = 5 ∧ (2 * (v / 3) + 1) % 3 = 0)
  1045        ∨ (v % 9 = 8 ∧ (2 * (v / 3) + 1) % 3 = 2) := by
  1046    have h9 : v % 9 = 2 ∨ v % 9 = 5 ∨ v % 9 = 8 := by omega
  1047    rcases h9 with h9 | h9 | h9
  1048    · exact Or.inl ⟨h9, by omega⟩
  1049    · exact Or.inr (Or.inl ⟨h9, by omega⟩)
  1050    · exact Or.inr (Or.inr ⟨h9, by omega⟩)
  1051
  1052  /-! ## The cross term, decomposed
  1053
  1054  Write each histogram as its flat part plus a deviation.  Because both deviations
  1055  sum to zero, the flat parts contribute exactly the product of the totals over the
  1056  modulus and nothing else survives to first order: the cross term IS the
  1057  correlation of the two deviations, with no remainder.  That is `cross_decompose`
  1058  below, stated over any finite index type and any reindexing of the second factor.
  1059
  1060  With it, Cauchy–Schwarz gives `|cross(r)| ≤ (3/8)·√(e_even(r)·e_odd(r))`, where
  1061  `e_even(r)` is the level's own excess exactly, by the doubling bijection, and
  1062  `e_odd(r)` is the arm image's.  Summed against the weight that is
  1063  `c ≤ (3/8)√3·√(‖e_arm‖/‖e‖)`.
  1064
  1065  Measured, the arm image is as uniform as its quarter share allows — the ratio runs
  1066  `3.7` to `5.7`, against the `4` that population alone would give — so the bound
  1067  lands at `1.25` to `1.55`.  The measured `c` is about `0.1`.  So Cauchy–Schwarz is
  1068  loose here by roughly a factor of fourteen, and it cannot reach `1/4`: it takes
  1069  absolute values, and the sign is exactly what carries the contraction.  The
  1070  inequality below is what is provable without the sign; the sign is what remains. -/
  1071
  1072  /-- The flat parts cancel: a cross sum of two mean-zero-perturbed histograms is
  1073      the product of the totals plus the correlation of the deviations, exactly. -/
  1074  theorem cross_decompose {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι]
  1075      (A B : ℚ) (a b : ι → ℚ) (σ : ι ≃ ι)
  1076      (ha : ∑ i, a i = 0) (hb : ∑ i, b i = 0) :
  1077      ∑ i, (A + a i) * (B + b (σ i))
  1078        = (Fintype.card ι : ℚ) * A * B + ∑ i, a i * b (σ i) := by
  1079    have hbσ : ∑ i, b (σ i) = 0 := by
  1080      rw [← hb]
  1081      exact Fintype.sum_equiv σ _ _ (fun _ => rfl)
  1082    have expand : ∀ i : ι, (A + a i) * (B + b (σ i))
  1083        = A * B + A * b (σ i) + a i * B + a i * b (σ i) := fun i => by ring
  1084    rw [Finset.sum_congr rfl (fun i _ => expand i)]
  1085    simp only [Finset.sum_add_distrib, ← Finset.mul_sum, ← Finset.sum_mul, ha, hbσ,
  1086      Finset.sum_const, Finset.card_univ, nsmul_eq_mul, mul_zero, zero_mul, add_zero,
  1087      zero_add]
  1088    ring
  1089
  1090  /-! ## Why the cross term is negative: the lag sum rule
  1091
  1092  Both deviations are the same level's, read under two affine maps, so the cross
  1093  term is an autocorrelation of the deviation at a nonzero lag.  A deviation sums to
  1094  zero, and that forces the autocorrelations over ALL lags to sum to zero:
  1095
  1096      Σ_lag R(lag) = (Σ_c d c)² = 0,   R 0 = ‖d‖² > 0
  1097
  1098  so the nonzero lags sum to `−‖d‖²`.  They cannot all be positive; on average they
  1099  are negative, and a typical one sits at `−‖d‖²/(M−1)`.  That is the whole source
  1100  of the sign, and `autocorr_sum_zero` proves it for any mean-zero function on a
  1101  finite additive group.
  1102
  1103  What it buys depends on the conductor.  Measured, the cross term runs `0.349` of
  1104  the Cauchy–Schwarz bound at conductor 9 and `0.189` at conductor 27 — which is
  1105  `3^(-r/2)` to two digits, square-root cancellation over the `3^r` lags.  At
  1106  conductor 3 there are only two nonzero lags and no such gain exists, and that is
  1107  exactly where the weight `3^(-r)` is heaviest.  So the low rungs cannot be handled
  1108  generically; they need their identities, and `r = 1` already has one — the exact
  1109  integer recursion `I_{d+1} = −I_d + (m₂ − m₈)`. -/
  1110
  1111  /-- A mean-zero function's autocorrelations sum to zero over all lags.  The zero
  1112      lag contributes `‖d‖²`, so every other lag together contributes `−‖d‖²`: the
  1113      nonzero lags cannot all be positive, which is where the cross term's sign
  1114      comes from. -/
  1115  theorem autocorr_sum_zero {G : Type*} [Fintype G] [DecidableEq G] [AddCommGroup G]
  1116      (d : G → ℚ) (hd : ∑ x, d x = 0) :
  1117      ∑ t, ∑ x, d x * d (x + t) = 0 := by
  1118    have inner : ∀ x : G, ∑ t, d x * d (x + t) = 0 := by
  1119      intro x
  1120      rw [← Finset.mul_sum]
  1121      have : ∑ t, d (x + t) = ∑ y, d y :=
  1122        Fintype.sum_equiv (Equiv.addLeft x) _ _ (fun _ => rfl)
  1123      rw [this, hd, mul_zero]
  1124    rw [Finset.sum_comm]
  1125    simpa using Finset.sum_congr rfl (fun x _ => inner x)
  1126
  1127  /-! ## Why the low conductors are the hard ones
  1128
  1129  The doubling permutation on residues mod `3^r` has order `2·3^(r-1)`: the kernel
  1130  reads `2, 6, 18, 54, 162` at `r = 1..5`.  That single fact organises everything
  1131  about the sign.
  1132
  1133  At `r = 1` the order is **two** — the permutation is the involution
  1134  `double_swaps_classes`, so a deviation alternates every level and the
  1135  cancellation is complete and per-level.  That is why the conductor-3 identity
  1136  `I_{d+1} = −I_d + (m₂ − m₈)` carries a bare minus sign, and why the cross term is
  1137  negative in 20 of 24 levels there.
  1138
  1139  At `r ≥ 2` the order is `6, 18, 54, …`.  There is no per-level sign at all; the
  1140  deviation returns to itself only after a full cycle, and what survives per level
  1141  is dilution rather than cancellation.  Measured, the cross term sits at `0.349`
  1142  of the Cauchy–Schwarz bound at conductor 9 and `0.189` at conductor 27, against
  1143  `3^(-r/2)` of `0.333` and `0.192` — square-root cancellation over the classes,
  1144  with the cycle length `2·3^(r-1)` proportional to that count.
  1145
  1146  So the two regimes are structurally different, and the split in the proof follows
  1147  the split in the order: the low rungs get exact identities, because they have a
  1148  sign to carry, and the high rungs get the generic square-root bound, because they
  1149  have length to average over. -/
  1150
  1151  theorem two_order_mod_three : 2 ^ 2 % 3 = 1 ∧ 2 ^ 1 % 3 ≠ 1 := by decide
  1152
  1153  theorem two_order_mod_nine :
  1154      2 ^ 6 % 9 = 1 ∧ 2 ^ 1 % 9 ≠ 1 ∧ 2 ^ 2 % 9 ≠ 1 ∧ 2 ^ 3 % 9 ≠ 1
  1155        ∧ 2 ^ 4 % 9 ≠ 1 ∧ 2 ^ 5 % 9 ≠ 1 := by decide
  1156
  1157  theorem two_order_mod_twentyseven :
  1158      2 ^ 18 % 27 = 1 ∧ 2 ^ 6 % 27 ≠ 1 ∧ 2 ^ 9 % 27 ≠ 1 := by decide
  1159
  1160  /-- At conductor three, and only there, doubling is its own inverse — which is
  1161      the whole of the per-level sign. -/
  1162  theorem doubling_involutive_only_at_three (m : ℕ) :
  1163      (4 * m) % 3 = m % 3 ∧ ¬ (∀ x : ℕ, (4 * x) % 9 = x % 9) := by
  1164    refine ⟨by omega, fun h => ?_⟩
  1165    have := h 1
  1166    omega
  1167
  1168  /-! ## The identity at conductor nine
  1169
  1170  At `r = 1` the doubling permutation is an involution and the recursion closes on
  1171  itself in one step.  At `r = 2` it is a six-cycle, so there is no per-level sign,
  1172  but the recursion is still exact — only now it reads the level at two resolutions
  1173  at once.
  1174
  1175  Both arms are determined:
  1176
  1177    * the even child `2m` has class `2·(m mod 9) mod 9`, and doubling is a bijection
  1178      there with inverse `5`, since `2·5 = 10 ≡ 1 (mod 9)`;
  1179    * the odd child `2t+1` of a junction `3t+2` has class `2·(t mod 9)+1 mod 9`, so
  1180      it is fixed by `t mod 9`, which is `m mod 27`.
  1181
  1182  So each class mod 9 of the next level is fed by exactly one class mod 9 of this
  1183  one through the doubling arm, and by exactly one class mod 27 through the odd arm.
  1184  `odd_source_mod_nine` names the second: the `t` class feeding `c` is `5·(c−1)`,
  1185  written `5·(c+8)` to stay in `ℕ`. -/
  1186
  1187  theorem even_child_mod_nine (m : ℕ) : (2 * m) % 9 = (2 * (m % 9)) % 9 := by omega
  1188
  1189  theorem odd_child_mod_nine (t : ℕ) : (2 * t + 1) % 9 = (2 * (t % 9) + 1) % 9 := by omega
  1190
  1191  theorem inv_two_mod_nine : (2 * 5) % 9 = 1 := by decide
  1192
  1193  /-- Doubling is onto the classes mod 9, so every class of the next level has
  1194      exactly one doubling source. -/
  1195  theorem doubling_onto_mod_nine : ∀ c < 9, ∃ b, b < 9 ∧ (2 * b) % 9 = c := by decide
  1196
  1197  /-- And the odd arm's source: the `t` class feeding class `c` is `5(c−1) mod 9`,
  1198      so the junction class feeding it is `3·that + 2` mod 27. -/
  1199  theorem odd_source_mod_nine : ∀ c < 9, (2 * ((5 * (c + 8)) % 9) + 1) % 9 = c := by decide
  1200

     3  // src/collatz.rs — the Collatz object as a nesting, read the way straus reads
     4  // the Erdős–Straus ladder.
     5  //
     6  /// The action is one BLOCK: from n, apply the shortcut map n/2 | (3n+1)/2 until
     7  /// the value first falls below n. Every block strictly decreases, so the nest is
     8  /// monotone in the value and one is the fixed point, held outright. Collatz is
     9  /// therefore a claim about the BUDGET on this nesting — how many blocks — and not
    10  /// about whether it arrives, exactly as Erdős–Straus is a budget on greedy
    11  /// removal. What is open sits inside the action rather than around it: a block
    12  /// closes when the first drop exists, and the depth split is the measurement of
    13  /// that.
    14  ///
    15  // Author: Quantum⊙perator (Lando⊗⊙perator team)
    16  extern crate alloc;
    17  use alloc::format;
    18  use alloc::string::String;
    19  use alloc::vec::Vec;
    20
    21  // f64_powi replacement for .powi() which is not stable on f64 in this toolchain
    22  fn f64_powi(x: f64, n: i32) -> f64 {
    23      let mut r = 1.0_f64;
    24      let mut b = x;
    25      let mut e = n;
    26      while e > 0 {
    27          if e & 1 == 1 { r *= b; }
    28          b *= b;
    29          e >>= 1;
    30      }
    31      r
    32  }
    33
    34  /// The shortcut map itself.
    35  pub fn step(n: u64) -> u64 {
    36      if n % 2 == 0 { n / 2 } else { (3 * n + 1) / 2 }
    37  }
    38
    39  /// One block: the first value below `n`, and what the block cost in steps.
    40  /// A block that does not close inside its allowance returns `None`, which is
    41  /// the open arm reporting itself rather than a wrong answer.
    42  pub fn block(n: u64, allowance: u32) -> Option<(u64, u32)> {
    43      if n <= 1 { return Some((1, 0)); }
    44      let mut v = n;
    45      let mut steps = 0u32;
    46      while steps < allowance {
    47          v = step(v);
    48          steps += 1;
    49          if v < n { return Some((v, steps)); }
    50      }
    51      None
    52  }
    53
    54  /// The budget: blocks to reach one, and the total shortcut steps they cost.
    55  pub fn budget(n: u64, allowance: u32) -> Option<(u32, u32, u64)> {
    56      let mut v = n;
    57      let mut blocks = 0u32;
    58      let mut total = 0u32;
    59      let mut peak = n;
    60      while v > 1 {
    61          let (next, cost) = block(v, allowance)?;
    62          // the peak is read inside the block, since that is where the value rises
    63          let mut w = v;
    64          for _ in 0..cost { w = step(w); if w > peak { peak = w; } }
    65          v = next;
    66          blocks += 1;
    67          total += cost;
    68      }
    69      Some((blocks, total, peak))
    70  }
    71
    72
    73  /// Natural log, since the kernel's float shims carry sqrt and exp but no ln and
    74  /// this is no_std. Range-reduce by powers of two, then the atanh series, which
    75  /// converges fast once the argument sits near one.
    76  pub fn f64_ln(x: f64) -> f64 {
    77      if x <= 0.0 { return 0.0; }
    78      const LN2: f64 = 0.693147180559945309417232121458;
    79      let mut y = x;
    80      let mut k = 0i32;
    81      while y >= 2.0 { y /= 2.0; k += 1; }
    82      while y < 1.0 { y *= 2.0; k -= 1; }
    83      // ln y = 2 atanh(t), t = (y-1)/(y+1)
    84      let t = (y - 1.0) / (y + 1.0);
    85      let t2 = t * t;
    86      let mut term = t;
    87      let mut sum = t;
    88      let mut n = 3.0f64;
    89      for _ in 0..40 {
    90          term *= t2;
    91          sum += term / n;
    92          n += 2.0;
    93      }
    94      2.0 * sum + k as f64 * LN2
    95  }
    96
    97
    98  /// Sine and cosine by Taylor with range reduction, since the kernel's float
    99  /// shims carry sqrt and exp and this is no_std. Only needed to a few ulp: the
   100  /// character sums below are read at three or four digits.
   101  const TAU: f64 = 6.283185307179586476925286766559;
   102
   103  pub fn f64_sin(x: f64) -> f64 {
   104      let mut t = x;
   105      while t > TAU / 2.0 { t -= TAU; }
   106      while t < -TAU / 2.0 { t += TAU; }
   107      let t2 = t * t;
   108      let mut term = t;
   109      let mut sum = t;
   110      let mut n = 1.0f64;
   111      for _ in 0..24 {
   112          n += 2.0;
   113          term *= -t2 / (n * (n - 1.0));
   114          sum += term;
   115      }
   116      sum
   117  }
   118
   119  pub fn f64_cos(x: f64) -> f64 { f64_sin(x + TAU / 4.0) }
   120
   121  pub struct Collatz;
   122
   123  impl Collatz {
   124      pub fn help() -> String {
   125          let mut s = String::from("collatz — the block nesting, and the budget it spends\n\n");
   126          s.push_str("The action is one BLOCK: from n, run the shortcut map until the value\n");
   127          s.push_str("first falls below n. Every block strictly decreases, so the nest is\n");
   128          s.push_str("monotone in the value and one is held outright. The conjecture is the\n");
   129          s.push_str("BUDGET on this nesting, not its arrival — the same shape `nesting`\n");
   130          s.push_str("assigns Erdős–Straus through `greedy`.\n\n");
   131          s.push_str("  collatz <n>              blocks, steps and peak for one n\n");
   132          s.push_str("  collatz trace <n>        every block, with the gap ratio it closes\n");

   166      pub fn one(n: u64) -> String {
   167          match budget(n, 100_000) {
   168              None => format!("collatz {}: a block exceeded its allowance — the arm is open here\n", n),
   169              Some((b, t, peak)) => {
   170                  let mut s = format!("collatz {}\n", n);
   171                  s.push_str(&format!("  blocks to one:   {}\n", b));
   172                  s.push_str(&format!("  shortcut steps:  {}\n", t));
   173                  s.push_str(&format!("  peak value:      {}   ({:.3}x the seed)\n",
   174                      peak, peak as f64 / n as f64));
   175                  s.push_str(&format!("  budget per bit:  {:.4}\n",
   176                      b as f64 / (64 - n.leading_zeros()) as f64));
   177                  s
   178              }
   179          }
   180      }
   181
   182      pub fn trace(n: u64) -> String {
   183          let mut s = format!("collatz trace {}\n", n);
   184          s.push_str("  block      from -> to     cost   gap ratio\n");
   185          let mut v = n;
   186          let mut i = 0;
   187          let mut prev_gap = (n - 1) as f64;
   188          while v > 1 && i < 512 {
   189              match block(v, 100_000) {
   190                  None => { s.push_str("  a block exceeded its allowance — open here\n"); break; }
   191                  Some((next, cost)) => {
   192                      let gap = (next - 1) as f64;
   193                      let q = if prev_gap > 0.0 { gap / prev_gap } else { 0.0 };
   194                      s.push_str(&format!("  {:>5}  {:>9} -> {:<9} {:>4}   {:.6}\n",
   195                          i + 1, v, next, cost, q));
   196                      prev_gap = gap;
   197                      v = next;
   198                      i += 1;
   199                  }
   200              }

   207      /// Where two trajectories first land on the same value. Two numbers with
   208      /// nothing to do with each other merge only in the tail every trajectory
   209      /// shares; two sitting on one branch of the predecessor tree merge at the
   210      /// branch point, high up and reached fast. The verb answers which.
   211      pub fn merge(a: u64, b: u64) -> String {
   212          let mut s = format!("collatz merge {} {}\n", a, b);
   213          let mut path_a: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
   214          let mut v = a;
   215          let mut guard = 0;
   216          while v != 1 && guard < 100_000 { path_a.push(v); v = step(v); guard += 1; }
   217          path_a.push(1);
   218          let mut v = b;
   219          let mut steps_b = 0u32;
   220          guard = 0;
   221          while guard < 100_000 {
   222              if let Some(i) = path_a.iter().position(|&x| x == v) {
   223                  s.push_str(&format!("  meet value:      {}\n", v));
   224                  s.push_str(&format!("  steps from {}:   {}\n", a, i));
   225                  s.push_str(&format!("  steps from {}:   {}\n", b, steps_b));
   226                  s.push_str(&format!("  shared tail:     {} of {} steps on the first path\n",
   227                      path_a.len() - i, path_a.len()));
   228                  s.push_str(&format!("  the meet sits:   {}\n",
   229                      if v > a.max(b) { "ABOVE both seeds — they join on the way up" }
   230                      else if v > a.min(b) { "above the smaller seed only" }
   231                      else { "below both — they join only in the common tail" }));
   232                  return s;
   233              }
   234              v = step(v);
   235              steps_b += 1;
   236          }
   237          s.push_str("  no meeting inside the allowance\n");
   238          s
   239      }
   240
   241
   242      /// The chain the budget records climb. Two backward steps — one doubling and
   243      /// one odd lift, the two arms of the split composed once each — send n to
   244      /// (4n-1)/3, defined exactly when n = 1 (mod 3). Consecutive records follow
   245      /// that rule wherever it stays defined, which is why their ratio sits at
   246      /// four thirds; where it fails the record switches chains instead.
   247      pub fn chain(n: u64) -> String {
   248          let mut s = format!("collatz chain {}\n", n);
   249          s.push_str("  n -> (4n-1)/3, one doubling and one odd lift\n\n");
   250          let mut v = n;
   251          let mut i = 0;
   252          while v % 3 == 1 && i < 64 {
   253              let next = (4 * v - 1) / 3;
   254              let b = budget(v, 100_000).map(|t| t.0).unwrap_or(0);
   255              let bn = budget(next, 100_000).map(|t| t.0).unwrap_or(0);
   256              s.push_str(&format!("  {:>12} (budget {:>3})  ->  {:>12} (budget {:>3})   ratio {:.6}\n",
   257                  v, b, next, bn, next as f64 / v as f64));
   258              v = next;
   259              i += 1;
   260          }
   261          s.push_str(&format!("  chain ends at {} — it is {} (mod 3), so the odd lift has no arm here\n",
   262              v, v % 3));
   263          s
   264      }
   265
   266
   267      /// The junction census. A value takes two predecessors exactly when it is
   268      /// 2 (mod 3) — `2v` on the even arm and `(2v-1)/3` on the odd one — so every
   269      /// merge in the tree happens there and nowhere else. What is not settled by
   270      /// that is which junctions carry the traffic, and how the two arms share it.
   271      /// This walks every seed in the range and counts arrivals at each junction
   272      /// by the arm they came in on.
   273      pub fn junctions(lo: u64, hi: u64, top: usize) -> String {
   274          let mut s = format!("collatz junctions {}..{}\n", lo, hi);
   275          // sparse counts: (value, even arrivals, odd arrivals)
   276          let mut keys: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
   277          let mut even: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
   278          let mut odd: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
   279          let mut seeds = 0u64;
   280          for n in lo..=hi {
   281              seeds += 1;
   282              let mut w = n;
   283              let mut guard = 0;
   284              while w != 1 && guard < 100_000 {
   285                  let v = step(w);
   286                  if v % 3 == 2 {
   287                      let from_even = w % 2 == 0 && w == 2 * v;
   288                      let idx = match keys.binary_search(&v) {
   289                          Ok(i) => i,
   290                          Err(i) => { keys.insert(i, v); even.insert(i, 0); odd.insert(i, 0); i }
   291                      };
   292                      if from_even { even[idx] += 1; } else { odd[idx] += 1; }
   293                  }
   294                  w = v;
   295                  guard += 1;
   296              }
   297          }
   298          let mut order: Vec<usize> = (0..keys.len()).collect();
   299          order.sort_by_key(|&i| core::cmp::Reverse(even[i] + odd[i]));
   300          s.push_str(&format!("  seeds walked:    {}\n", seeds));
   301          s.push_str(&format!("  junctions used:  {}\n\n", keys.len()));
   302          s.push_str("       junction     traffic     even arm      odd arm   odd share\n");
   303          let mut tot_e: u64 = 0;
   304          let mut tot_o: u64 = 0;
   305          for &i in order.iter() { tot_e += even[i]; tot_o += odd[i]; }
   306          for &i in order.iter().take(top) {
   307              let t = even[i] + odd[i];
   308              s.push_str(&format!("  {:>13}  {:>10}  {:>11}  {:>11}  {:>10.4}\n",
   309                  keys[i], t, even[i], odd[i], odd[i] as f64 / t as f64));
   310          }
   311          // the shape of the split across junctions, not just its total: a
   312          // junction that carries traffic on one arm only is doing no mixing,
   313          // whatever the global share says.
   314          let mut bins = [0u64; 10];
   315          let mut weight = [0u64; 10];
   316          for i in 0..keys.len() {
   317              let t = even[i] + odd[i];
   318              if t == 0 { continue; }
   319              let sh = odd[i] as f64 / t as f64;
   320              let mut b = (sh * 10.0) as usize;
   321              if b > 9 { b = 9; }
   322              bins[b] += 1;
   323              weight[b] += t;
   324          }
   325          s.push_str("\n  odd share    junctions      traffic\n");
   326          for b in 0..10 {
   327              s.push_str(&format!("  {:.1}-{:.1}  {:>12}  {:>11}\n",
   328                  b as f64 / 10.0, (b + 1) as f64 / 10.0, bins[b], weight[b]));
   329          }
   330          s.push_str(&format!("\n  all junctions:   even {}   odd {}   odd share {:.4}\n",
   331              tot_e, tot_o, tot_o as f64 / (tot_e + tot_o) as f64));
   332          s.push_str("  the odd share is the fraction of arrivals that used the arm only\n");
   333          s.push_str("  a 2 (mod 3) value has; the even arm is always available.\n");
   334          s
   335      }
   336
   337
   338      /// How many predecessors sit under a value, out to a given depth. The
   339      /// backward step is `2m` always, and `(2m-1)/3` when `m = 2 (mod 3)`; the
   340      /// edge back into the root cycle is cut so the count is of the tree rather
   341      /// than of the loop.
   342      pub fn subtree(start: u64, depth: u32) -> u64 {
   343          let mut level: Vec<u64> = alloc::vec![start];
   344          let mut total: u64 = 1;
   345          for _ in 0..depth {
   346              let mut next: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
   347              for &m in level.iter() {
   348                  if m <= u64::MAX / 2 { next.push(2 * m); }
   349                  if m % 3 == 2 {
   350                      let u = (2 * m - 1) / 3;
   351                      if u != 1 && u % 2 == 1 { next.push(u); }
   352                  }
   353              }
   354              total += next.len() as u64;
   355              level = next;
   356              if level.is_empty() { break; }
   357          }
   358          total
   359      }
   360
   361      /// What makes a junction balanced. Traffic measured from a window of seeds
   362      /// is a fact about the window; the intrinsic quantity is how much tree feeds
   363      /// each arm. This counts both subtrees to a common depth and reports the
   364      /// share the odd arm holds.
   365      pub fn balance(v: u64, depth: u32) -> String {
   366          let mut s = format!("collatz balance {} at depth {}\n", v, depth);
   367          if v % 3 != 2 {
   368              s.push_str(&format!("  {} is {} (mod 3) — one arm only, so it is no junction\n",
   369                  v, v % 3));
   370              return s;
   371          }
   372          let even_arm = 2 * v;
   373          let odd_arm = (2 * v - 1) / 3;
   374          let e = Self::subtree(even_arm, depth);
   375          let o = Self::subtree(odd_arm, depth);
   376          s.push_str(&format!("  even arm  {:>12}   subtree {:>10}\n", even_arm, e));
   377          s.push_str(&format!("  odd arm   {:>12}   subtree {:>10}\n", odd_arm, o));
   378          s.push_str(&format!("  odd share {:.4}\n", o as f64 / (e + o) as f64));
   379          s
   380      }
   381
   382      /// Scan the junctions for the balanced ones. Balance is intrinsic here, read
   383      /// off the two subtrees rather than off a seed window, so the answer does
   384      /// not move when the window does.
   385      pub fn balanced(lo: u64, hi: u64, depth: u32) -> String {
   386          let mut s = format!("collatz balanced {}..{} at depth {}\n", lo, hi, depth);
   387          s.push_str("  junctions whose two arms feed within a tenth of each other\n\n");
   388          s.push_str("       junction     even arm      odd arm   odd share\n");
   389          let mut seen = 0u64;
   390          let mut hits = 0u64;
   391          let mut share_sum = 0.0f64;
   392          let mut spread = [0u64; 10];
   393          for v in lo..=hi {
   394              if v % 3 != 2 { continue; }
   395              seen += 1;
   396              let e = Self::subtree(2 * v, depth);
   397              let o = Self::subtree((2 * v - 1) / 3, depth);
   398              let sh = o as f64 / (e + o) as f64;
   399              share_sum += sh;
   400              let mut b = (sh * 10.0) as usize;

   402       9  const FORK_STACK_CAP: usize = 64;
   403      10
   404      11  #[derive(Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
   405      12  pub enum Phase { Boot, Think, Act, Observe, Update, Halt }
   406      13
   407      14  /// A fork frame pushed when FSPLIT bifurcates execution.
   408      15  /// Tracks the two parallel branches until FFUSE joins them.
   409      16  #[derive(Copy, Clone)]
   410      17  struct ForkFrame {
   411      18      /// Position just after the matching FFUSE (where to resume after join).
   412      19      resume_ip: usize,
   413      20      /// The value carried on the right branch (gated by EVALT/EVALF).
   414      21      right_val: B4,
   415      22      /// Whether the right branch has been populated.
   416      23      right_set: bool,
   417      24  }
   418      25
   419      26  /// Structural snapshot computed by IMSCRIB.
   420      27  /// Dynamic fields (b_live_ticks, gate_discriminations, value_period) are
   421      28  /// overlaid from runtime accumulators after the static classification.
   422      29  #[derive(Copy, Clone, PartialEq)]
   423      30  pub struct Snapshot {
   424      31      pub frobenius_order: u8,
   425      32      pub period: usize,
   426      33      pub sig: (usize, usize, usize, usize), // (L, F, D, X)
   427      34      pub token_diversity: usize,
   428      35      pub self_ref: bool,
   429      36      pub dialetheia_complete: bool,
   430      37      pub tier: u8,
   431      38      // ── Dynamic (runtime) fields ──
   432      39      pub b_live_ticks: u64,           // ticks where B was on stack when EVALT or EVALF fired
   433      40      pub gate_discriminations: u64,   // ticks where EVALT actually passed T, or EVALF passed F
   434      41      pub value_period: usize,         // measured period of stack-top value trace (0 = not yet known)
   435      42      // ── SIXTEEN_3 / R2 fields (O_inf_dag, lateral replicative opening) ──
   436      43      // Mirror the Lean kernel's R2 triple (dim=dead, top=mime, prot=ah) as three
   437      44      // independent structural/runtime conditions rather than one boolean, per the
   438      45      // discerning ob3ect run (2026-07-15): atomicity, bifurcation, winding.
   439      46      pub atomic_reentry: bool,        // "dim=dead": exactly one FSPLIT/FFUSE pair — a
   440      47                                        // point-like fork, not an elaborate nested structure
   441      48      pub bifurcation_revisited: bool, // "top=mime": that single fork point recurs every wrap
   442      49                                        // (bowtie/figure-8), distinct from mere periodicity
   443      50      pub winding_count: u32,          // "prot=ah": protected integer winding — a monotonic
   444      51                                        // full-program-pass counter that never resets
   445      52  }
   446      53
   447      54  impl Snapshot {
   448      55      pub fn tier_name(self) -> &'static str {
   449      56          match self.tier {
   450      57              1 => "O_1", 2 => "O_2", 3 => "O_inf",
   451      58              4 => "O_inf_dag", // LATERAL to O_inf, not above it — see compute_tier
   452      59              _ => "O_0",
   453      60          }
   454      61      }
   455      62
   456      63      /// The ⊥ mirror: exchange the R1 (O_∞) and R2 (O_inf_dag) evidence triples,
   457      64      /// role for role, leaving the shared substrate (self_ref, frobenius_order,
   458      65      /// period, value_period, sig, diversity) untouched — the two dialects sit on
   459      66      /// one temporal substrate; only the evidence changes hands:
   460      67      ///   static mark:   dialetheia_complete      ↔ atomic_reentry
   461      68      ///   counted act:   b_live_ticks             ↔ winding_count
   462      69      ///   recurrence:    gate_discriminations > 0 ↔ bifurcation_revisited
   463      70      /// The count↔bool leg passes through the canonical section (true ↦ 1),
   464      71      /// so mirrored() alone is involutive on the witness plane, not on raw
   465      72      /// counts. The exact involution is Kernel::arev_hop, which is parity over
   466      73      /// unchanged accumulators — the door, not the mirror.
   467      74      pub fn mirrored(self) -> Snapshot {
   468      75          let mut m = self;
   469      76          m.dialetheia_complete = self.atomic_reentry;
   470      77          m.atomic_reentry      = self.dialetheia_complete;
   471      78          m.b_live_ticks        = self.winding_count as u64;
   472      79          m.winding_count       = self.b_live_ticks.min(u32::MAX as u64) as u32;
   473      80          m.gate_discriminations   = if self.bifurcation_revisited { 1 } else { 0 };
   474      81          m.bifurcation_revisited  = self.gate_discriminations > 0;
   475      82          m.tier = compute_tier(&m);
   476      83          m
   477      84      }
   478      85  }
   479      86
   480      87  /// Graph-execution kernel.
   481      88  /// FSPLIT creates fork frames. FFUSE joins them.
   482      89  /// Program is inherently cyclic: end wraps to start.
   483      90  /// TANCH at root depth sinks the wire -> halt.
   484      91  pub struct Kernel {
   485      92      pub program:     Program,
   486      93      pub ip:          usize,
   487      94      pub phase:       Phase,
   488      95      pub tick_count:  u64,
   489      96      pub memory:      B4Memory,
   490      97      pub stack:       B4Stack,
   491      98      pub registers:   B4Registers,
   492      99      pub snapshot:    Option<Snapshot>,
   493     100      pub frob_checks: u64,
   494     101      pub frob_open:   u64,
   495     102      pub harness:     FrobeniusHarness,
   496     103      fork_stack:      [ForkFrame; FORK_STACK_CAP],
   497     104      fork_depth:      usize,
   498     105      pub fork_overflow: u64,   // FSPLITs that exceeded FORK_STACK_CAP (0 in practice)
   499     106      pub halted:      bool,
   500     107      pub dynamic_mode: bool,  // true → rebuild program from IgTuple each wrap
   501     108      // ── Cross-dialect ruleset state ──
   502     109      pub active_dialect: u8,        // 0-87, current active ruleset (default 0 = canonical)
   503     110      /// ⊥ (chirality): false = or' reading (R1-dominant, the canonical hand),
   504     111      /// true = flipped — the kernel reads its own snapshot through the mirror
   505     112      /// (O_∞ ↔ O_inf_dag lateral hop, the ob3ect's AREV step 15). Toggled by
   506     113      /// arev_hop(); parity over unchanged accumulators, so hop∘hop = id exactly.
   507     114      pub chirality: bool,
   508     115      pub liminal_target: Option<u8>, // dialect jumped to, pending IFIX seal
   509     116      pub liminal_compound: Option<u8>,   // compound index (0-10) used for liminal jump
   510     117      // ── Runtime accumulators for dynamic snapshot fields ──
   511     118      b_live_count:             u64,
   512     119      gate_discrimination_count: u64,
   513     120      value_trace:              [B4; 16],   // ring buffer of stack-top values after each tick
   514     121      value_trace_head:         usize,
   515     122      winding_count:            u32,   // protected: incremented on every natural end-of-program
   516     123                                        // wrap, never reset (not even by disable_dynamic)
   517     124  }
   518     125
   519     126  impl Kernel {
   520     127      pub fn new() -> Self {
   521     128          Self {
   522     129              program:     bootstrap_loop(),
   523     130              ip:          0,
   524     131              phase:       Phase::Boot,
   525     132              tick_count:  0,
   526     133              memory:      B4Memory::new(),
   527     134              stack:       B4Stack::new(),
   528     135              registers:   B4Registers::new(),
   529     136              snapshot:    None,
   530     137              frob_checks: 0,
   531     138              frob_open:   0,
   532     139              harness:     FrobeniusHarness::new("mOMonadOS"),
   533     140              fork_stack:  [ForkFrame { resume_ip: 0, right_val: B4::N, right_set: false }; FORK_STACK_CAP],
   534     141              fork_depth:  0,
   535     142              fork_overflow: 0,
   536     143              halted:      false,
   537     144              dynamic_mode: false,
   538     145              active_dialect:      0,
   539     146              chirality:           false,
   540     147              liminal_target:       None,
   541     148              liminal_compound:     None,
   542     149              b_live_count:             0,
   543     150              gate_discrimination_count: 0,
   544     151              value_trace:              [B4::N; 16],
   545     152              value_trace_head:         0,
   546     153              winding_count:            0,
   547     154          }
   548     155      }
   549     156
   550     157      pub fn boot(&mut self) {
   551     158          self.snapshot = Some(self_imscribe(&self.program));
   552     159          self.phase = Phase::Think;
   553     160      }
   554     161
   555     162      fn in_fork(&self) -> bool { self.fork_depth > 0 }
   556     163      pub fn fork_depth(&self) -> usize { self.fork_depth }
   557     164
   558     165      fn push_fork(&mut self, resume_ip: usize) {
   559     166          if self.fork_depth < FORK_STACK_CAP {
   560     167              self.fork_stack[self.fork_depth] = ForkFrame {
   561     168                  resume_ip,
   562     169                  right_val: B4::N,
   563     170                  right_set: false,
   564     171              };
   565     172              self.fork_depth += 1;
   566     173          } else {
   567     174              // Fork stack full: record the overflow rather than dropping the fork
   568     175              // silently (a silent drop would desync the matching FFUSE). This path
   569     176              // is unreachable for real programs; it makes a pathological nest
   570     177              // observable instead of invisible.
   571     178              self.fork_overflow += 1;
   572     179          }
   573     180      }
   574     181
   575     182      fn pop_fork(&mut self) -> Option<ForkFrame> {
   576     183          if self.fork_depth > 0 {
   577     184              self.fork_depth -= 1;
   578     185              Some(self.fork_stack[self.fork_depth])
   579     186          } else {
   580     187              None
   581     188          }
   582     189      }
   583     190
   584     191      fn fork_top_mut(&mut self) -> Option<&mut ForkFrame> {
   585     192          if self.fork_depth > 0 {
   586     193              Some(&mut self.fork_stack[self.fork_depth - 1])
   587     194          } else {
   588     195              None
   589     196          }
   590     197      }
   591     198

   594  [INFO]   VERIFY: [closed] (read returned content — Frobenius closed)

   626     221      /// Find matching FFUSE3 for FSPLIT3 at split_ip via balanced parenthesis scan.
   627     222      pub fn find_matching_ffuse3(&self, split_ip: usize) -> usize {
   628     223          let mut depth = 1u32;
   629     224          let n = self.program.len();
   630     225          if n == 0 { return 0; }
   631     226          let mut i = (split_ip + 1) % n;
   632     227          let start = i;
   633     228          loop {
   634     229              match self.program.get(i) {
   635     230                  Some(Token::Fsplit3) => depth += 1,
   636     231                  Some(Token::Ffuse3)  => {
   637     232                      depth -= 1;
   638     233                      if depth == 0 { return i; }
   639     234                  }
   640     235                  _ => {}
   641     236              }
   642     237              i = (i + 1) % n;
   643     238              if i == start { break; }
   644     239          }
   645     240          n // unmatched
   646     241      }

   648     243      /// One Frobenius tick. Returns false if halted.
   649     244      pub fn tick(&mut self) -> bool {
   650     245          if self.phase == Phase::Halt || self.halted { return false; }
   651     246          self.tick_count += 1;
   652     247
   653     248          // THINK
   654     249          self.phase = Phase::Think;
   655     250          // ── Use dynamic_imscribe so tier reflects runtime behavior ──
   656     251          self.snapshot = Some(self.dynamic_imscribe());
   657     252          self.maybe_promote();
   658     253
   659     254          // ACT
   660     255          self.phase = Phase::Act;
   661     256          if self.ip >= self.program.len() {
   662     257              self.ip = 0;
   663     258              self.try_self_modify();
   664     259          }
   665     260          let tok = self.program.get(self.ip).unwrap();
   666     261
   667     262          let mut next_ip = self.ip + 1;
   668     263          if next_ip >= self.program.len() {
   669     264              next_ip = 0;
   670     265              // Natural full-program wrap — protected winding, never reset. FFUSE's
   671     266              // jump-to-resume below can also land next_ip at 0, but that's a fork
   672     267              // resume, not a completed pass, so it does not increment this.
   673     268              self.winding_count = self.winding_count.saturating_add(1);
   674     269          }
   675     270
   676     271          match tok {
   677     272              Token::Vinit => {
   678     273                  self.stack.push(B4::N);
   679     274              }
   680     275              Token::Tanch => {
   681     276                  let val = self.stack.pop();
   682     277                  let addr = self.registers.read(0) as usize;
   683     278                  self.memory.write(addr, val);
   684     279                  if !self.in_fork() {
   685     280                      self.phase = Phase::Halt;
   686     281                      self.halted = true;
   687     282                      return false;
   688     283                  }
   689     284              }
   690     285              Token::Afwd => {
   691     286                  let r0 = self.registers.read(0) as u8;
   692     287                  self.registers.write(0, B4::from_u8(r0.wrapping_add(1)));
   693     288              }
   694     289              Token::Arev => {
   695     290                  let r0 = self.registers.read(0) as u8;
   696     291                  self.registers.write(0, B4::from_u8(r0.wrapping_sub(1)));
   697     292              }
   698     293              Token::Clink => {
   699     294                  let a = self.registers.read(1);
   700     295                  let b = self.registers.read(2);
   701     296                  self.registers.write(3, b4_meet(a, b));
   702     297              }
   703     298              Token::Imscrib => {
   704     299                  if let Some(snap) = self.snapshot {
   705     300                      self.registers.write(4, B4::from_u8(snap.token_diversity as u8 & 3));
   706     301                      self.registers.write(5, if snap.self_ref           { B4::T } else { B4::F });
   707     302                      self.registers.write(6, if snap.frobenius_order > 0 { B4::T } else { B4::F });
   708     303                      self.registers.write(7, if snap.dialetheia_complete { B4::T } else { B4::F });
   709     304                  }
   710     305              }
   711     306              Token::Fsplit => {
   712     307                  let v = self.stack.peek();
   713     308                  let ffuse_ip = self.find_matching_ffuse(self.ip);
   714     309                  let resume = if ffuse_ip + 1 >= self.program.len() { 0 }
   715     310                               else { ffuse_ip + 1 };
   716     311                  self.push_fork(resume);
   717     312                  if let Some(frame) = self.fork_top_mut() {
   718     313                      frame.right_val = v;
   719     314                      frame.right_set = true;
   720     315                  }
   721     316                  self.stack.push(v);
   722     317              }
   723     318              Token::Evalt => {
   724     319                  let v = self.stack.pop();
   725     320                  // ── B-live instrumentation: B on stack when gate fires ──
   726     321                  if v == B4::B { self.b_live_count += 1; }
   727     322                  let filtered = if v == B4::T { B4::T } else { B4::N };
   728     323                  // ── Gate discrimination: T actually passed ──
   729     324                  if v == B4::T { self.gate_discrimination_count += 1; }
   730     325                  self.stack.push(filtered);
   731     326              }
   732     327              Token::Evalf => {
   733     328                  let v = self.stack.pop();
   734     329                  // ── B-live instrumentation ──
   735     330                  if v == B4::B { self.b_live_count += 1; }
   736     331                  let filtered = if v == B4::F { B4::F } else { B4::N };
   737     332                  // ── Gate discrimination: F actually passed ──
   738     333                  if v == B4::F { self.gate_discrimination_count += 1; }
   739     334                  self.stack.push(filtered);
   740     335              }
   741     336              Token::Ffuse => {
   742     337                  let left = self.stack.pop();
   743     338                  if let Some(frame) = self.pop_fork() {
   744     339                      let right = if frame.right_set { frame.right_val } else { B4::N };
   745     340                      self.stack.push(b4_join(left, right));
   746     341                      next_ip = frame.resume_ip;
   747     342                  } else {
   748     343                      self.stack.push(left);
   749     344                  }
   750     345              }
   751     346              Token::Engagr => {
   752     347                  self.registers.engagr = true;
   753     348                  self.stack.push(B4::B);
   754     349              }
   755     350              Token::Ifix => {
   756     351                  let addr = self.registers.read(0) as usize;
   757     352                  let val  = self.stack.pop();
   758     353                  self.memory.write(addr, val);
   759     354              }
   760     355              Token::Fsplit3 => {
   761     356                  // 3-way fork (T/F/I arms). In B4 context, same as Fsplit.
   762     357                  let v = self.stack.peek();
   763     358                  let ffuse_ip = self.find_matching_ffuse3(self.ip);
   764     359                  let resume = if ffuse_ip + 1 >= self.program.len() { 0 }
   765     360                               else { ffuse_ip + 1 };
   766     361                  self.push_fork(resume);
   767     362                  if let Some(frame) = self.fork_top_mut() {
   768     363                      frame.right_val = v;
   769     364                      frame.right_set = true;
   770     365                  }
   771     366                  self.stack.push(v);
   772     367              }
   773     368              Token::Ffuse3 => {
   774     369                  // 3-way fuse. In B4 context, same as Ffuse.
   775     370                  let left = self.stack.pop();
   776     371                  if let Some(frame) = self.pop_fork() {
   777     372                      let right = if frame.right_set { frame.right_val } else { B4::N };
   778     373                      self.stack.push(b4_join(left, right));
   779     374                      next_ip = frame.resume_ip;
   780     375                  } else {
   781     376                      self.stack.push(left);
   782     377                  }
   783     378              }
   784     379              Token::Evali => {
   785     380                  // Evaluate on Information axis: keep B (both), everything else -> N.
   786     381                  let v = self.stack.pop();
   787     382                  self.stack.push(if v == B4::B { B4::B } else { B4::N });
   788     383              }
   789     384              Token::Rotat => {
   790     385                  // ROTAT — the first op-opcode: cyclic shift of the program-ring
   791     386                  // by k, here realized as stack rotation. Pops k (default 1) and
   792     387                  // rotates the remaining stack data by k positions mod depth.
   793     388                  // The ring automorphism: ROTAT^n = identity.
   794     389                  let k_val = self.stack.pop();
   795     390                  let k = (k_val as u8 as usize).max(1);
   796     391                  self.stack.rotate(k);
   797     392              }
   798     393          }
   799     394
   800     395          self.ip = next_ip;
[INFO]   VERIFY: [closed] (read returned content — Frobenius closed)
[INFO]   UPDATE: file_read → Frobenius closed B4=B DIALETHEIC
[INFO] ── Winding 4 [23:42:01Z]  ctx:32k/128k (26%) ──────────────────────

  ⟨file_read⟩ {"path":"/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/wew.txt","offset":800}

   804     399          self.value_trace_head = (self.value_trace_head + 1) % 16;

   839     422
   840     423      /// Run N ticks (tight loop).
   841     424      pub fn run(&mut self, max_ticks: u64) -> u64 {
   842     425          let start = self.tick_count;
   843     426          while !self.halted && (self.tick_count - start) < max_ticks {
   844     427              self.tick();
   845     428          }
   846     429          self.tick_count - start
   847     430      }
   848     431
   849     432      /// Continuous execution. Returns when halted or should_stop() true.
   850     433      pub fn run_continuous<F: FnMut() -> bool>(&mut self, mut should_stop: F) -> u64 {
   851     434          let start = self.tick_count;
   852     435          while !self.halted && !should_stop() {
   853     436              self.tick();
   854     437          }
   855     438          self.tick_count - start
   856     439      }
   857     440
   858     441      /// Run one tick if the timer has fired.
   859     442      pub fn tick_on_timer(&mut self) -> bool {
   860     443          if crate::interrupts::timer_ready() {
   861     444              crate::interrupts::pending_ticks();
   862     445              self.tick()
   863     446          } else {
   864     447              !self.halted
   865     448          }
   866     449      }
   867     450
   868     451      /// Install `prog` and zero runtime evidence so a subsequent `snapshot`
   869     452      /// reports the *static* classification of the new program (not the previous
   870     453      /// program's b_live / winding / stale tier). Without this, VII→XII handoffs
   871     454      /// (and any `load` before the first tick) left the old snapshot in place.
   872     455      /// Stack and registers are cleared so leftover B from a prior program cannot
   873     456      /// fake b_live on the next one.
   874     457      fn install_program(&mut self, prog: Program) {
   875     458          self.program = prog;
   876     459          self.ip = 0;
   877     460          self.fork_depth = 0;
   878     461          self.halted = false;
   879     462          self.phase = Phase::Think;
   880     463          self.b_live_count = 0;
   881     464          self.gate_discrimination_count = 0;
   882     465          self.value_trace = [B4::N; 16];
   883     466          self.value_trace_head = 0;
   884     467          self.winding_count = 0;
   885     468          self.stack.clear();
   886     469          self.registers.clear();
   887     470          self.snapshot = Some(self_imscribe(&self.program));
   888     471      }
   889     472
   890     473      pub fn load_canonical(&mut self, idx: usize) {
   891     474          if let Some(prog) = canonical(idx) {
   892     475              self.install_program(prog);
   893     476          }
   894     477      }
   895     478
   896     479      /// Load the program that deliberately targets O_inf_dag (R2, lateral replicative
   897     480      /// opening) instead of terminal closure — see `tokens::replicative_opening_loop` for why
   898     481      /// this specific 4-token cycle avoids both O_∞ paths by construction. Ticking it past its
   899     482      /// first wrap (4 ticks) is what actually sets `winding_count > 0`; loading alone only
   900     483      /// gets you the two structural preconditions (atomic_reentry, bifurcation_revisited).
   901     484      pub fn load_replicative(&mut self) {
   902     485          self.install_program(crate::tokens::replicative_opening_loop());
   903     486      }
   904     487
   905     488      pub fn load_continuous(&mut self, idx: usize) -> bool {
   906     489          if let Some(prog) = continuous_program(idx) {
   907     490              self.install_program(prog);
   908     491              true
   909     492          } else {
   910     493              false
   911     494          }
   912     495      }
   913     496
   914     497      pub fn load_novel(&mut self, idx: usize) -> bool {
   915     498          if let Some(prog) = novel_program(idx) {
   916     499              self.install_program(prog);
   917     500              true
   918     501          } else {
   919     502              false
   920     503          }
   921     504      }
   922     505
   923     506      pub fn load_shunted(&mut self, idx: usize) -> bool {
   924     507          if let Some(prog) = shunted_program(idx) {
   925     508              self.install_program(prog);
   926     509              true
   927     510          } else {
   928     511              false
   929     512          }
   930     513      }
   931     514
   932     515      pub fn load_compound(&mut self, idx: usize) -> bool {
   933     516          if let Some(prog) = compound_program(idx) {
   934     517              self.install_program(prog);
   935     518              true
   936     519          } else {
   937     520              false
   938     521          }
   939     522      }
   940     523
   941     524      pub fn halt(&mut self) { self.phase = Phase::Halt; self.halted = true; }
   942     525
   943     526      fn maybe_promote(&mut self) {
   944     527          if let Some(snap) = self.snapshot {
   945     528              let old = snap.tier;
   946     529              let new = compute_tier(&snap);
   947     530              if new != old {
   948     531                  if let Some(s) = self.snapshot.as_mut() { s.tier = new; }
   949     532              }
   950     533          }
   951     534      }
   952     535
   953     536      fn try_self_modify(&mut self) {
   954     537          if self.dynamic_mode {
   955     538              // Derive next program from current IgTuple rather than running a preset.
   956     539              let snap = self.dynamic_imscribe();
   957     540              let tuple = crate::imas_ig::IgTuple::from_snapshot(&snap);
   958     541              let len = crate::sequence::next_seq_len(&snap);
   959     542              self.program = crate::sequence::build_via_substrate(&tuple, len, snap.self_ref, snap.tier);
   960     543              self.snapshot = Some(self_imscribe(&self.program));
   961     544              self.ip = 0;
   962     545              self.fork_depth = 0;
   963     546          } else if self.stack.depth() > 200 {
   964     547              self.program.inject(self.ip, Token::Tanch);
   965     548              self.snapshot = Some(self_imscribe(&self.program));
   966     549          }
   967     550      }
   968     551
   969     552      /// Enable dynamic mode: the kernel rebuilds its own sequence from its
   970     553      /// current IgTuple each time the program wraps. The first sequence is
   971     554      /// built from the current snapshot (or bootstrap defaults if no snapshot yet).
   972     555      pub fn load_dynamic(&mut self) {
   973     556          self.dynamic_mode = true;
   974     557          let snap = match self.snapshot {
   975     558              Some(s) => s,
   976     559              None    => self_imscribe(&self.program),
   977     560          };
   978     561          let tuple = crate::imas_ig::IgTuple::from_snapshot(&snap);
   979     562          let len = crate::sequence::next_seq_len(&snap);
   980     563          self.program = crate::sequence::build_via_substrate(&tuple, len, snap.self_ref, snap.tier);
   981     564          self.snapshot = Some(self_imscribe(&self.program));
   982     565          self.ip = 0;
   983     566          self.fork_depth = 0;

  1230      47  /* ── Crystal encoding ──────────────────────────────────────────────── */
  1231      48  static const int PRIM_CARD[12] = {4,5,4,5,3,5,3,4,5,4,3,4};
  1232      49
  1233      50  /* O_inf tuple (canonical Shavian per spec):
  1234      51     ⟨𐑦·𐑸·𐑾·𐑹·𐑐·𐑧·𐑲·𐑠·⊙·𐑫·𐑳·𐑭⟩
  1235      52     Indices: D T R P F K G ∋ ⊙ H S ◻ */
  1236      53  static const int O_INF_VALS[12] = {3,4,3,4,2,2,2,2,1,2,0,2};
  1237      54
  1238      55  static int crystal_encode(const int vals[12]) {
  1239      56      int strides[12];
  1240      57      strides[11] = 1;
  1241      58      for (int i = 10; i >= 0; i--)
  1242      59          strides[i] = strides[i + 1] * PRIM_CARD[i + 1];
  1243      60      int addr = 0;
  1244      61      for (int i = 0; i < 12; i++)
  1245      62          addr += vals[i] * strides[i];
  1246      63      return addr;
  1247      64  }
  1248      65
  1249      66  static void crystal_decode(int addr, int out[12]) {
  1250      67      int strides[12];
  1251      68      strides[11] = 1;
  1252      69      for (int i = 10; i >= 0; i--)
  1253      70          strides[i] = strides[i + 1] * PRIM_CARD[i + 1];
  1254      71      for (int i = 0; i < 12; i++) {
  1255      72          out[i] = addr / strides[i];
  1256      73          addr %= strides[i];
  1257      74      }
  1258      75  }
  1259      76
  1260      77  /* ── Glyph format ──────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
  1261      78  static const char *GLYPH_STR[12][5] = {
  1262      79      /* D  */ {"𐑛", "𐑨", "𐑼", "𐑦"},
  1263      80      /* T  */ {"𐑡", "𐑰", "𐑥", "𐑶", "𐑸"},
  1264      81      /* R  */ {"𐑩", "𐑑", "𐑽", "𐑾"},
  1265      82      /* P  */ {"𐑗", "𐑿", "𐑬", "𐑯", "𐑹"},
  1266      83      /* F  */ {"𐑱", "𐑞", "𐑐"},
  1267      84      /* K  */ {"𐑘", "𐑤", "𐑧", "𐑪", "𐑺"},
  1268      85      /* G  */ {"𐑚", "𐑔", "𐑲"},
  1269      86      /* ∋  */ {"𐑝", "𐑜", "𐑠", "𐑵"},
  1270      87      /* ⊙  */ {"𐑢", "⊙", "𐑮", "𐑻", "𐑣"},
  1271      88      /* H  */ {"𐑓", "𐑒", "𐑖", "𐑫"},
  1272      89      /* S  */ {"𐑙", "𐑕", "𐑳"},
  1273      90      /* ◻  */ {"𐑷", "𐑴", "𐑭", "𐑟"},
  1274      91  };
  1275      92
  1276      93  static void format_type(const int vals[12], char *buf) {
  1277      94      char *p = buf;
  1278      95
  1279      96      /* Canonical Shavian notation per shavian_notation_spec.md:
  1280      97       * ⟨𐑛·𐑡·𐑩·𐑗·𐑱·𐑘·𐑚·𐑝·𐑢·𐑓·𐑙·𐑷⟩
  1281      98       * No family names, no underscores — just the 12 specific glyphs separated by · inside ⟨⟩.
  1282      99       */
  1283     100      *p++ = 0xE2; *p++ = 0x9F; *p++ = 0xA8;   /* ⟨ U+27E8 */
  1284     101
  1285     102      for (int i = 0; i < 12; i++) {
  1286     103          if (i > 0) {
  1287     104              *p++ = 0xC2; *p++ = 0xB7;        /* · U+00B7 middle dot */
  1288     105          }
  1289     106          /* Belt-and-suspenders clamp */
  1290     107          int maxg = 0;
  1291     108          while (GLYPH_STR[i][maxg]) maxg++;
  1292     109          int gidx = vals[i] % maxg;
  1293     110          if (gidx < 0) gidx = 0;
  1294     111          const char *glyph = GLYPH_STR[i][gidx];
  1295     112          while (*glyph) *p++ = *glyph++;
  1296     113      }
  1297     114
  1298     115      *p++ = 0xE2; *p++ = 0x9F; *p++ = 0xA9;   /* ⟩ U+27E9 */
  1299     116      *p = '\0';
  1300     117  }
  1301     118
  1302     119  static const char *ouroborics_tier(const int vals[12]) {
  1303     120      if (vals[8] == 1 && vals[11] >= 2 && vals[0] == 3) return "O_inf";
  1304     121      if (vals[8] == 1 && vals[11] >= 1 && vals[0] >= 2) return "O_2";
  1305     122      if (vals[8] == 1) return "O_1";
  1306     123      return "O_0";
  1307     124  }

  1309     126  /* ── Meet (pointwise min) ──────────────────────────────────────────── */
  1310     127  static void meet_vals(const int a[12], const int b[12], int out[12]) {
  1311     128      for (int i = 0; i < 12; i++)
  1312     129          out[i] = (a[i] < b[i]) ? a[i] : b[i];
  1313     130  }
  1314     131
  1315     132  /* ── Integer to string ─────────────────────────────────────────────── */
  1316     133  static void itoa_simple(int n, char *buf) {
  1317     134      char *p = buf;
  1318     135      if (n == 0) { *p++ = '0'; *p = 0; return; }
  1319     136      char tmp[16]; int i = 0;
  1320     137      while (n) { tmp[i++] = '0' + (n % 10); n /= 10; }
  1321     138      while (i) *p++ = tmp[--i];
  1322     139      *p = 0;
  1323     140  }

  1407     212  /* ── Phase 2: Service Imscription ──────────────────────────────────── */
  1408     213  static void service_imscription(ServiceDef *svcs, int n) {
  1409     214      serial_print("  Services inscribed into the crystal:\n\n");
  1410     215      for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
  1411     216          char buf[256]; format_type(svcs[i].vals, buf);
  1412     217          serial_print("  ["); serial_print(svcs[i].tier <= 0 ? "O_0" :
  1413     218                  svcs[i].tier == 1 ? "O_1" : svcs[i].tier == 2 ? "O_2" :
  1414     219                  "O_inf");
  1415     220          serial_print("] "); serial_print(svcs[i].name);
  1416     221          serial_print("\n      "); serial_print(buf);
  1417     222          serial_print("\n");
  1418     223      }
  1419     224  }
  1420     225
  1421     226  /* ── Phase 3: Meet-Semilattice Dependencies ────────────────────────── */
  1422     227  static void resolve_deps(ServiceDef *svcs, int n) {
  1423     228      int dep_found = 0;
  1424     229      serial_print("  Dependency edges (meet above O_0 floor):\n\n");
  1425     230      for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
  1426     231          for (int j = i + 1; j < n; j++) {
  1427     232              int m[12];
  1428     233              meet_vals(svcs[i].vals, svcs[j].vals, m);
  1429     234              int above_floor = 0;
  1430     235              for (int k = 0; k < 12; k++)
  1431     236                  if (m[k] > 0) { above_floor = 1; break; }
  1432     237              if (!above_floor) continue;
  1433     238
  1434     239              for (int k = 0; k < 12; k++) {
  1435     240                  if (m[k] < svcs[i].vals[k]) {
  1436     241                      serial_print("  "); serial_print(svcs[i].name);
  1437     242                      serial_print("  -->  "); serial_print(svcs[j].name);
  1438     243                      serial_print("\n");
  1439     244                      dep_found++; break;
  1440     245                  }
  1441     246              }
  1442     247              for (int k = 0; k < 12; k++) {
  1443     248                  if (m[k] < svcs[j].vals[k]) {
  1444     249                      serial_print("  "); serial_print(svcs[j].name);
  1445     250                      serial_print("  -->  "); serial_print(svcs[i].name);
  1446     251                      serial_print("\n");
  1447     252                      dep_found++; break;
  1448     253                  }
  1449     254              }
  1450     255          }
  1451     256      }
  1452     257      char db[16]; itoa_simple(dep_found, db);
  1453     258      serial_print("  Total edges: "); serial_print(db); serial_print("\n");
  1454     259  }

  1456     261  /* ── Phase 4: Tier-Ladder Boot Order ───────────────────────────────── */
  1457     262  static void tier_ladder_order(ServiceDef *svcs, int n) {
  1458     263      /* Sort by tier ascending */
  1459     264      for (int i = 1; i < n; i++) {
  1460     265          ServiceDef key = svcs[i];
  1461     266          int j = i - 1;
  1462     267          while (j >= 0 && svcs[j].tier > key.tier) {
  1463     268              svcs[j + 1] = svcs[j]; j--;
  1464     269          }
  1465     270          svcs[j + 1] = key;
  1466     271      }
  1467     272      serial_print("  Boot sequence (tier-ladder):\n\n");
  1468     273      for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
  1469     274          char nb[4]; itoa_simple(i + 1, nb);
  1470     275          serial_print("  "); serial_print(nb); serial_print(". [");
  1471     276          serial_print(svcs[i].tier <= 0 ? "O_0" :
  1472     277                  svcs[i].tier == 1 ? "O_1" : svcs[i].tier == 2 ? "O_2" :
  1473     278                  "O_inf");
  1474     279          serial_print("] "); serial_print(svcs[i].name);
  1475     280          serial_print("\n");
  1476     281      }
  1477     282  }

  1479     284  /* ── Phase 5: Service Activation ───────────────────────────────────── */
  1480     285  static void activate_services(ServiceDef *svcs, int n) {
  1481     286      for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
  1482     287          serial_print("  START [");
  1483     288          serial_print(svcs[i].tier <= 0 ? "O_0" :
  1484     289                  svcs[i].tier == 1 ? "O_1" : svcs[i].tier == 2 ? "O_2" :
  1485     290                  "O_inf");

  1490     295           * then immediately ENGAGR it toward B (the stable contradiction). */

  1492     297              svcs[i].belief[k] = svcs[i].vals[k];
  1493     298          }

  1495     300          svcs[i].belief[8] = 1;   /* ⊙ toward _ÿ (critical) */
  1496     301          svcs[i].belief[11] = 2;  /* ◻ toward z */

  1504     309              for (int k=0; k<8; k++) svcs[i].program[k] = p[k];
  1505     310          } else if (nm[0] == 'n') {  /* nginx - mid tier balancer */

  1510     315              for (int k=0; k<8; k++) svcs[i].program[k] = p[k];

  1516     321              for (int k=0; k<8; k++) svcs[i].program[k] = p[k];

  1519     324              for (int k=0; k<8; k++) svcs[i].program[k] = p[k];

  1523     328
  1524     329  /* ── IMASM stepper: each service runs its own tiny self-imscribing program ─
  1525     330   * This is the bare-metal heart: services literally execute small loops of
  1526     331   * the core 12-opcode grammar (ENGAGR, FSPLIT, FFUSE, IFIX, IMSCRIB...).
  1527     332   * All steps are constructed so the global meet remains closed. */
  1528     333  static void service_imasm_step(ServiceDef *s) {
  1529     334      if (s->pc >= 8) s->pc = 0;
  1530     335      uint8_t op = s->program[s->pc];
  1531     336      s->pc = (s->pc + 1) % 8;
  1532     337
  1533     338      switch (op) {
  1534     339          case OP_ENGAGR:
  1535     340              /* Engage the critical point */
  1536     341              s->belief[8] = 1;   /* ⊙ */
  1537     342              s->belief[11] = 2;  /* ◻ toward stable Z */
  1538     343              break;
  1539     344
  1540     345          case OP_FSPLIT:
  1541     346              /* Branch belief (co-multiplication) */
  1542     347              s->belief[0] = (s->belief[0] + 1) % PRIM_CARD[0];
  1543     348              s->belief[4] = (s->belief[4] + 1) % PRIM_CARD[4];  /* ⋈ */
  1544     349              break;
  1545     350
  1546     351          case OP_FFUSE:
  1547     352              /* Recombine using lattice meet (stand-in for B4 join) */
  1548     353              {
  1549     354                  int fused = (s->belief[0] > s->belief[3]) ? s->belief[0] : s->belief[3];
  1550     355                  s->belief[0] = fused;
  1551     356                  s->belief[3] = fused;
  1552     357              }
  1553     358              break;
  1554     359
  1555     360          case OP_IFIX:
  1556     361              /* Fix the critical coordinate */
  1557     362              s->belief[8] = 1;
  1558     363              break;
  1559     364
  1560     365          case OP_IMSCRIB:
  1561     366              /* Self-imscription: recognize current state */
  1562     367              s->belief[11] = 2;
  1563     368              break;
  1564     369
  1565     370          case OP_AREV:
  1566     371              /* Reverse / reflection — the dangerous path for ⊢ (card 4) swapping with < (card 5) */
  1567     372              {
  1568     373                  int t = s->belief[0];
  1569     374                  int src = s->belief[3] % PRIM_CARD[0];  /* clamp source before writing to ⊢ */
  1570     375                  s->belief[0] = src;
  1571     376                  s->belief[3] = t;
  1572     377              }
  1573     378              break;
  1574     379
  1575     380          case OP_NOP:
  1576     381          default:
  1577     382              break;
  1578     383      }
  1579     384
  1580     385      /* Critical: always normalize after mutation so no coordinate ever exceeds its alphabet size.
  1581     386       * This prevents the off-by-one glyph table read that was producing mangled
  1582     387       * trailing bytes when AREV (or similar) wrapped a high value from a
  1583     388       * larger-cardinality primitive into the Dimensionality slot. */
  1584     389      for (int k = 0; k < 12; k++) {
  1585     390          if (s->belief[k] < 0) s->belief[k] = 0;
  1586     391          s->belief[k] %= PRIM_CARD[k];
  1587     392      }
  1588     393
  1589     394      s->local_paradox += 1;
  1590     395  }
  1591     396
  1592     397  /* Legacy name for scheduler calls */
  1593     398  static void service_frobenius_step(ServiceDef *s) {
  1594     399      service_imasm_step(s);
  1595     400  }

  1609     402  /* ── Recompute the global running meet from all live service beliefs ──
  1610     403   * Core rule: pointwise min (meet), but then aggressively re-engage the
  1611     404   * O_inf critical coordinates (⊢ high, ⊙ critical, ◻ high). This lets the
  1612     405   * system sustain or recover higher tiers even when lower services pull
  1613     406   * some dimensions down. This is the "ascent" / maintenance of the grammar
  1614     407   * in the live scheduler. */
  1615     408  static void recompute_global_meet(ServiceDef *svcs, int n, int out[12]) {
  1616     409      for (int k = 0; k < 12; k++) out[k] = O_INF_VALS[k];   /* start from kernel O_inf */
  1617     410      for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
  1618     411          meet_vals(out, svcs[i].belief, out);
  1619     412      }
  1620     413
  1621     414      /* Re-engage O_inf invariants (the "ascent" maintenance) */
  1622     415      out[0]  = (out[0]  > 2) ? out[0]  : 3;   /* ⊢ toward ω (high) */
  1623     416      out[8]  = 1;                              /* ⊙ must be critical */
  1624     417      out[11] = (out[11] > 1) ? out[11] : 2;   /* ◻ toward z */
  1625     418  }

  2051       1  ; paradoxd v0.11 — Multiboot (v1) header + entry trampoline
  2052       2  ; Uses classic Multiboot v1. No VBE video request — QEMU doesn't support it.
  2053       3
  2054       4  [bits 32]
  2055       5
  2056       6  section .multiboot_header
  2057       7  align 4
  2058       8
  2059       9  mb_header_start:
  2060      10      dd 0x1BADB002          ; multiboot v1 magic
  2061      11      dd 0x00000003          ; flags: bit0=align_modules, bit1=meminfo
  2062      12                             ; bit2 (video) NOT set — QEMU can't handle VBE
  2063      13      dd 0xE4524FFB          ; checksum: -(0x1BADB002 + 0x00000003) = 0xE4524FFB
  2064      14      ; A.out kludge fields (all zero for ELF format)
  2065      15      dd 0                   ; header_addr (unused for ELF)
  2066      16      dd 0                   ; load_addr (unused for ELF)
  2067      17      dd 0                   ; load_end_addr (unused for ELF)
  2068      18      dd 0                   ; bss_end_addr (unused for ELF)
  2069      19      dd _start32            ; entry_addr
  2070      20      ; NOTE: No video mode fields — bit 2 is clear
  2071      21  mb_header_end:
  2072      22
  2073      23  section .text
  2074      24  global _start32
  2075      25  extern _start
  2076      26
  2077      27  _start32:
  2078      28      ; GRUB drops us in 32-bit protected mode with interrupts disabled.
  2079      29      ; EAX = 0x2BADB002 (Multiboot magic), EBX = multiboot_info_t pointer.
  2080      30      ; First, write a test character to VGA text buffer to confirm we got here
  2081      31      mov dword [0xB8000], 0x0F210F48  ; "HI!" in white on black at top-left
  2082      32
  2083      33      mov esp, stack_top
  2084      34      cld
  2085      35      ; Align stack to 16 bytes (required by System V ABI)
  2086      36      and esp, 0xFFFFFFF0
  2087      37      ; Push multiboot info pointer as arg to _start
  2088      38      push ebx
  2089      39      call _start
  2090      40      add esp, 4
  2091      41
  2092      42  .hang:
  2093      43      hlt
  2094      44      jmp .hang
  2095      45
  2096      46  section .bss
  2097      47  align 16
  2098      48  stack_bottom:
  2099      49      resb 16384
  2100      50  stack_top:

  4602      22  /-- Number of odd steps taken in the first `k` steps from `r`. -/
  4603      23  def oddSteps : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ
  4604      24    | _, 0     => 0
  4605      25    | r, (k+1) => (if r % 2 = 0 then 0 else 1) + oddSteps (col r) k
  4606      26
  4607      27  /-- A residue class contracts when its multiplier loses to its divisor. -/
  4608      28  def Contracts (k r : ℕ) : Prop := 3 ^ oddSteps r k < 2 ^ k
  4609      29
  4610      30  instance (k r : ℕ) : Decidable (Contracts k r) := by unfold Contracts; infer_instance

  4612      32  /-! ## The shift theorem: the iterate is affine on each class -/
  4613      33
  4614      34  theorem col_shift : ∀ (k t r : ℕ),
  4615      35      col^[k] (2 ^ k * t + r) = 3 ^ oddSteps r k * t + col^[k] r := by
  4616      36    intro k
  4617      37    induction k with
  4618      38    | zero => intro t r; simp [oddSteps]
  4619      39    | succ k ih =>
  4620      40      intro t r
  4621      41      have hp : (2:ℕ) ^ (k+1) * t = 2 * (2 ^ k * t) := by ring
  4622      42      rw [Function.iterate_succ_apply, Function.iterate_succ_apply]
  4623      43      rcases Nat.even_or_odd r with h | h
  4624      44      · obtain ⟨m, hm⟩ := h
  4625      45        have hr2 : r % 2 = 0 := by omega
  4626      46        have hstep : col (2 ^ (k+1) * t + r) = 2 ^ k * t + col r := by
  4627      47          have hpar : (2 ^ (k+1) * t + r) % 2 = 0 := by rw [hp]; omega
  4628      48          unfold col
  4629      49          rw [if_pos hpar, if_pos hr2, hp]
  4630      50          omega
  4631      51        rw [hstep, ih t (col r)]
  4632      52        rw [oddSteps, if_pos hr2, Nat.zero_add]
  4633      53      · obtain ⟨m, hm⟩ := h
  4634      54        have hr2 : r % 2 = 1 := by omega
  4635      55        have hstep : col (2 ^ (k+1) * t + r) = 2 ^ k * (3 * t) + col r := by
  4636      56          have hpar0 : ¬ ((2 ^ (k+1) * t + r) % 2 = 0) := by rw [hp]; omega
  4637      57          have hr0 : ¬ (r % 2 = 0) := by omega
  4638      58          unfold col
  4639      59          rw [if_neg hpar0, if_neg hr0]
  4640      60          have h3 : 3 * (2 ^ (k+1) * t + r) + 1 = 2 * (2 ^ k * (3 * t)) + (3 * r + 1) := by
  4641      61            rw [hp]; ring
  4642      62          rw [h3]; omega
  4643      63        rw [hstep, ih (3 * t) (col r)]
  4644      64        rw [oddSteps, if_neg (by omega : ¬ r % 2 = 0)]
  4645      65        ring

  4647      67  /-! ## Contraction from the coordinate -/
  4648      68
  4649      69  /-- A class with gap `m` below `2^k` contracts for every `t ≥ 1` once its
  4650      70      constant `col^[k] r` sits within `m` of `r`. -/
  4651      71  theorem col_lt_of_gap {k r m : ℕ} (hm : 3 ^ oddSteps r k + m = 2 ^ k)
  4652      72      (hc : col^[k] r < r + m) :
  4653      73      ∀ t, 1 ≤ t → col^[k] (2 ^ k * t + r) < 2 ^ k * t + r := by

  4665      85      col (3 ^ i * 2 ^ (m+1) - 1) = 3 ^ (i+1) * 2 ^ m - 1 := by
  4666      86    have hM : 3 ^ i * 2 ^ (m+1) = 2 * (3 ^ i * 2 ^ m) := by ring
  4667      87    have hpos : 1 ≤ 3 ^ i * 2 ^ m := Nat.one_le_iff_ne_zero.mpr (by positivity)
  4668      88    have hnext : 3 ^ (i+1) * 2 ^ m = 3 * (3 ^ i * 2 ^ m) := by ring

  4803     211  /-- Contraction at depth `i` is a property of the class mod `2^i`. -/

  4808     216  /-! ## Survivors: classes that contract at no depth up to k -/
  4809     217
  4810     218  /-- `r` survives to depth `k` when no depth `i ≤ k` contracts it. -/
  4811     219  def Survives (k r : ℕ) : Prop := ∀ i, i ≤ k → ¬ Contracts i r
  4812     220
